/bin/bash -lc "timeout 20s sed -n '647,883p' llp/3000.017-ephemeral-tables.rfc.md" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4
 succeeded in 0ms:
### 3.4 Generation fence, restart, adoption

The memory store is bound to the `(store identity, generation)` it serves.

- Every hub access runs under the hub lock, for mutations and for queries.
  It compares the durable snapshot's `(store identity, generation)`, which
  its read transaction has already established, with the hub's binding:
  - **hub older:** clear it, rebind to the snapshot's pair, and change the
    incarnation;
  - **snapshot older:** refuse with the store's retryable generation
    refusal. The request retries on a fresh reader.
- Adoption (`serve_adoption.rs`) holds the engine mutex. In-place activation
  checks the hub for poison, releases it throughout the SQLite transaction,
  then takes it to clear and rebind after commit. It never holds the hub while
  waiting for SQLite readers: those readers take SQLite before the hub, and a
  DELETE-mode writer needs them to finish before commit or spill. A hub poisoned
  after the initial check is completely reset at committed rebind, avoiding a
  post-commit refusal (LLP 3000.033 §1.3). Engine replacement (`open_engine_at`)
  likewise clears and rebinds the shared hub before publishing the new engine.
- A reader loaned before adoption can therefore never write under the old
  generation's indexes into the new store, nor read new rows under old
  rules.

Ephemeral rows do not survive any of these:
- a server restart;
- schema adoption under `dev`;
- a generation change;
- a store replacement.

Each changes the incarnation, so every client watching an ephemeral table
re-runs its queries (§3.3 fencing).

Day-two classification (`successor.rs`):
- **Destructive:** a durable table gaining `ephemeral`. Activation deletes
  its stored rows and index entries even though the table name remains
  (`store/activation.rs` today deletes only disappeared names).
- **Additive:**
  - an ephemeral table becoming durable, as a new, empty table;
  - adding an ephemeral table;
  - removing one;
  - changing one.

The ephemeral case must be decided before the generic "removed; its rows
are lost" branch (`successor.rs:65-69`). The `$deadline` engine-field
branch is not the only step.

### 3.5 Bounds

| Bound | Value | Refusal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Life | 1 s – 60 s | `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL` at `check`; generation admission re-checks |
| Live rows written by one principal | 1,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes written by one principal | 4 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Live rows, all principals (backstop) | 100,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes, all principals (backstop) | 512 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Ephemeral mutations per session | 20 per second sustained, burst 40 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` |
| Waiting calls in a client's lane | 256 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` (local) |
| Rows per group | the table's `cap … by …` | the existing cap refusal |
| Work per mutation and per query | the existing meter | unchanged |

Every refusal in this table is retryable, carries no row detail, and
arrives as HTTP 200 `{ state: "failed", why }` (§2.4 rule 5).

**How capacity is counted.**
- The hub records each row's writing principal.
- A row is charged its **declared maximum image size**, computed from the
  schema's bounds for its columns and index keys, never its actual bytes.
- Per-principal admission therefore depends only on the writer's own rows
  and on the schema. Another principal's private payload cannot flip it
  (Astra F3).
- The global backstop reveals only a server-wide aggregate. That is the
  same residual class as HTTP admission being full.

**When capacity is checked.**
- Capacity counts live rows only. The hub sweeps expired rows before it
  refuses.
- The check runs under the hub lock, after the program and before the epoch
  bump. A refusal undoes the journal: the row is absent and the epoch does
  not move.

**The rate bucket is new.**
- It is a new per-session token bucket, checked before the mutation runs.
- It applies under `dev` and `serve` alike.
- It shares nothing with the login and upload counters that `E_RATE_LIMIT`
  names today. The code is reused, its card text is extended, and its
  family stays `auth`.

The lane's coalescing and renewal window keep an ordinary typing indicator
near one request per second per typist. That is far below these bounds.

### 3.6 What does not change, and what the lock claims are

**What does not change.**
- A schema with no ephemeral table serializes byte-identically. Its wire,
  its device and the published-0.2.33 capability fences are unchanged.
- A new capability name, `ephemeral`, makes an older device refuse a schema
  that has one, instead of queuing its writes.
- `connected[p]` keeps its meaning and its grace.
- Durable mutations, their prediction, the outbox, replay and the partition
  log are unchanged.
- Presence-guarded effects are unchanged.

**The lock claims.** An ephemeral mutation:
- never opens a SQLite write transaction;
- never holds the serving engine's mutex while it executes;
- takes that mutex only for the short sections a query takes: session
  resolution and reader checkout.

Only queries and mutations whose static read closure includes an ephemeral
table take the hub lock, and only for their metered run. Message pages and
other durable queries never wait on it. `MemoryRows` is `!Sync`, so this is
a `Mutex`, not an `RwLock`; the programs that take it are small.

## 4. Implementation

The work runs in two codex `gpt-6-astra` xhigh lanes on the Apartment M5 Pro
mini, from `origin/main` at launch:

- **E1** — compiler, core, engine, server, device guard, guide cards;
  branch `sb4/eph-engine`.
- **E2** — the TypeScript client; branch `sb4/eph-client`, started from
  E1's server checkpoint so that its tests run the real CLI.

File references are from main `71f77c16ca`. The lanes re-verify each
citation.

### 4.1 Language (`crates/snapback4-lang`)

- `ast.rs` `TableDecl` (327-344): `ephemeral: Option<EphemeralDecl { life_ms, site }>`.
- `parser.rs` table body (188-313): add an `ephemeral` arm beside `expire`
  (236-251) and `online` (252-260), reusing `parser/views.rs` `duration_unit`.
- `bind/schema.rs`:
  - `bind_ephemeral` beside `bind_expire` (236-245, 270-283);
  - the refusals of §2.1 in `bind_table` (133-265), `probe` (514) for
    every table, `bind_sync` (285+) and reference columns;
  - the hidden `$deadline` column and `$expiry` index, mirroring
    `bind/through.rs` (218-248).
- A new exhaustive effect visitor (for example `bind/effects.rs`, shared
  with `engine/read_only.rs` if that fits):
  - it classifies programs and computes `renews` and `ephemeralReads`;
  - it drives §2.2's refusals from `bind/mod.rs` (122-140);
  - it sets `ephemeral`, `renews`, `renewMs` and `predicts = "never"` on
    mutation facts, and `ephemeralReads` on query facts;
  - it also produces the `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER` note.
- `bind/residency.rs` (`site` 181-202, `table_line` 494-503,
  `operation_lines` 505-535): the ephemeral residency line.
- `emit.rs` (61-66, 98-104; the import at 19): the facts of §2.4, and the
  `Ephemeral` import when used.
- `guide.rs` `KEYWORDS` (57): the `ephemeral` card and the `typing` alias.
- `guide/codes.rs`:
  - add `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` and `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`;
  - extend `E_RATE_LIMIT`'s text to ephemeral writes;
  - amend `E_OFFLINE` (193).
- `guide/reads.rs` (95-130), `guide/presence.rs` and `tests/guide.rs`:
  rewrite `w10b_typing_card_compiles_and_expires_at_the_deadline`
  (229-244) for the ephemeral recipe, and keep a durable `expire at` test.

### 4.2 Core schema (`crates/snapback4-core`)

- `schema.rs` `Table` (217-259): `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] ephemeral: Option<Ephemeral { life_ms: u64 }>`.
  Use no `untagged` or `flatten`, because the wasm family census refuses
  them.
- `Execution` (53-80): `ephemeral`, `renews` and `renew_ms` on mutations,
  `ephemeral_reads` on queries. Each has a serde default and is skipped when
  false or empty. The wire names are camelCase, as the other facts'
  are.
- `schema/through.rs` (26-45): `is_expiring`, `expiry_column` and
  `engine_field` include ephemeral tables, whose deadline is `$deadline`.
- `schema.rs` `expired` / `expiry_index` (305-318) follow.
- `engine/recorded.rs` (20-23) and `value/disclosed.rs` (152-159, 219-233):
  hide `$deadline` (§3.1).
- `schema/capabilities.rs` (37-49): `("ephemeral", |s| s.tables.values().any(|t| t.ephemeral.is_some()))`.
- Generation admission: re-check the life bound and §2.1's refusals, so a
  hand-edited schema cannot bypass the compiler.
- `successor.rs` (65-135) and `store/activation.rs` (116-125): §3.4.

### 4.3 Engine

A new `engine/ephemeral.rs` (gated on the `sqlite` feature, like the server
engine) adds three things.

- **`Hub`** is shared by `Arc` between the serving engine and its readers.
  It holds:
  - `Mutex<HubState>`, containing:
    - `MemoryRows` (`rows.rs:282-425`);
    - the `(store identity, generation)` binding;
    - per-row writer and declared size;
    - per-principal and global counters;
    - `epoch`, `incarnation` and the ring;
  - `sweep(now)`, which reclaims memory;
  - `clear_and_rebind(identity, generation)`, which also changes the
    incarnation.
- **`Layered<'a> { durable: &dyn Rows, eph: &MemoryRows, journal, mode }`**
  implements `Rows` by routing on the table name.
  - Ephemeral tables go to `eph`, and every mutation of `eph` is
    journaled.
  - Everything else goes to `durable`: durable tables, sequences,
    `effect_once`, assets and prefetch.
  - In ephemeral-mutation mode, any durable write is an internal refusal.
  - A drop guard undoes the journal unless the commit completed (§2.2).
- **Queries.** Only a query whose static `ephemeralReads` is non-empty runs
  over `Layered` and takes the hub lock. It holds the lock for its metered
  run. All other queries run exactly as today.

**`mutate_ephemeral`.** `Engine::mutate` checks the operation's `ephemeral`
fact right after its kind checks (`engine/mod.rs` 946-1058). It then
branches to `mutate_ephemeral`, which:

1. Opens a **read** transaction on the durable store (`read_transaction`,
   deferred), never `transaction()`. A pooled reader engine does this, and
   the serving engine's mutex is not held while it runs.
2. Locks the hub and applies §3.4's generation check.
3. Runs the program over `Layered`, stamping `$deadline` and applying the
   renewal law in `write.rs`:
   - at insert, after defaults (58-62, before validation at 127);
   - at update, as its own assignment beside `recompute_deadline` (281).
4. On success, checks §3.5's capacity (undoing and refusing if exceeded),
   bumps the epoch and records the written tables. On any other exit, the
   drop guard undoes the journal.
5. Publishes the wake while still holding the hub (hub, then wake mutex;
   §3.3), then releases the hub.
6. Writes nothing to `_commits`, `_log`, `_outbox` or `_ops`.

It returns the result, the epoch, the incarnation and the written tables.

**Sweeps.** `sweep_expired_for` (`engine/mod.rs:780-820`) skips ephemeral
tables. The hub's `sweep` runs from the maintenance loop.

**Other paths.**
- `seed_at`, `load` and `/seed` refuse ephemeral tables.
- `snapback4 test` uses the in-process hub, so `test` can exercise the
  typing recipe.
- `snapback4 run` of an ephemeral mutation prints a note: the row lives in
  this process only, and a running `dev` server's clients will not see it.
- The Postgres store's `supported()` (`postgres/mod.rs:396-421`) refuses a
  schema with an ephemeral table until increment C3.

//! A campaign fork retains activated semantics without running adoption again.
use super::*;
impl Engine {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#122-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — isolation includes entropy, assets and ephemeral ownership.
    pub fn fork_fixture(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        if self.presence.is_some(){return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("fixture fork needs an isolated external presence snapshot".into()));}
        let store=self.store.fork_memory()?;
        let identity=store.meta("store_id")?.ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Store("fixture store has no identity".into()))?;
        Ok(Self {
            timeline_schedule:timeline::Schedule::default(),
            ephemeral:std::sync::Arc::new(if self.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.ephemeral.is_some()){self.ephemeral.fork_fixture(&identity,self.generation)?}else{ephemeral::Hub::new(identity,self.generation,&self.schema)}),
            store,schema:self.schema.clone(),programs:self.programs.clone(),ceilings:self.ceilings,
            entropy:crate::ids::seed(),minter:crate::ids::Mint::default(),generation:self.generation,
            expiry_after:self.expiry_after.clone(),dev_effects:self.dev_effects,
            #[cfg(feature="native")] native:self.native.clone(),
            #[cfg(feature="native")] native_logs:native::Logs::default(),presence:None,
        })
    }
}

impl Engine {
    /// Judge independent fixture intent with the engine's authorized rule hops.
    pub fn fixture_policy(&mut self,rule:&crate::schema::RuleExpr,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,next:&Value,now:i64)->Result<rules::Verdict,RuntimeError>{
        let tx=self.store.read_transaction()?;
        let context=if viewer==crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL{Context::system()}else{Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new())};
        let mut runner=Runner::new(&tx,&self.schema,context.at(now),Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(),Cut::Abort),0);
        let bytes=runner.rule_scratch(rule);
        runner.authorized_scratch(bytes,&[],|runner,allowance|{
            let mut probe=read::RuleProbe{runner,allowance};
            rules::evaluate_verdict(rule,viewer,old,next,false,&mut probe)
        })
    }
}
//! One process's short-lived rows and commit epoch, shared by every reader.
// @ref LLP 3000.017#4.3 — no SQLite writer, log, replay record or durable receipt.

use super::*;
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir, MemoryRows, Rows};
pub use crate::schema::ephemeral::Version;
use crate::schema::{Schema, Table};
pub use layered::Layered;
use std::{
    collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet, VecDeque},
    sync::{Arc, Mutex, MutexGuard},

pub const RING_SIZE: usize = 256;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct Change {
    pub preserved: Vec<String>,
    pub epoch: u64,
    pub incarnation: String,
    pub tables: Vec<String>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Snapshot {
    pub preserved: VecDeque<(u64, Vec<String>)>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub generation: u64,
    pub version: Version,
    pub ring: VecDeque<(u64, Vec<String>)>,
    pub tables: Vec<String>,
}
impl Snapshot {
    pub fn preserved_since(&self, epoch: u64, incarnation: &str) -> Vec<String> {
        if incarnation != self.version.incarnation || epoch >= self.version.epoch { return vec![]; }
        let mut result: Option<Vec<String>> = None;
        let mut previous = epoch;
        for (at, tables) in self.preserved.iter().filter(|(at,_)| *at > epoch) {
            if *at != previous + 1 { return vec![]; }
            if let Some(result) = &mut result { result.retain(|t| tables.contains(t)); } else { result = Some(tables.clone()); }
            previous = *at;
        }
        if previous != self.version.epoch { return vec![]; }
        result.unwrap_or_default()
    }

    pub fn changed(
        &self,
        epoch: u64,
        incarnation: Option<&str>,
        interest: Option<&BTreeSet<String>>,
    ) -> Vec<String> {
        let allowed = |name: &String| interest.is_none_or(|set| set.contains(name));
        if incarnation.is_some_and(|id| id != self.version.incarnation)
            || epoch > self.version.epoch
            || self
                .ring
                .front()
                .is_some_and(|(first, _)| epoch.saturating_add(1) < *first)
        {
            return match interest {
                Some(tables) => tables.iter().cloned().collect(),
                None => self.tables.clone(),
            };
        }
        self.ring
            .iter()
            .filter(|(at, _)| *at > epoch)
            .flat_map(|(_, tables)| tables.iter())
            .filter(|t| allowed(t))
            .cloned()
            .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()
            .into_iter()
            .collect()
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Limits {
    pub principal_rows: u64,
    pub principal_bytes: u64,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub bytes: u64,
}
impl Default for Limits {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            principal_rows: 1000,
            principal_bytes: 4 * 1024 * 1024,
            rows: 100_000,
            bytes: 512 * 1024 * 1024,
        }
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)]
struct Count {
    rows: u64,
    bytes: u64,
}
impl Count {
    fn add(&mut self, bytes: u64) {
        self.rows += 1;
        self.bytes = self.bytes.saturating_add(bytes);
    }
    fn remove(&mut self, bytes: u64) {
        self.rows -= 1;
        self.bytes -= bytes;
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
struct Accounting {
    owners: BTreeMap<(String, String), (String, u64)>,
    principals: BTreeMap<String, Count>,
    total: Count,
}
struct Plan {
    changes: Vec<((String, String), Option<(String, u64)>)>,
    principals: BTreeMap<String, Count>,
    total: Count,
}
impl Accounting {
    fn remove(&mut self, key: &(String, String)) {
        if let Some((principal, bytes)) = self.owners.remove(key) {
            self.total.remove(bytes);
            let count = self.principals.get_mut(&principal).unwrap();
            count.remove(bytes);
            if count.rows == 0 {
                self.principals.remove(&principal);
            }
        }
    }
    fn plan(
        &self,
        layer: &Layered<'_>,
        schema: &Schema,
        viewer: &str,
        limits: Limits,
    ) -> Result<Plan, RuntimeError> {
        let mut plan = Plan {
            changes: Vec::new(),
            principals: BTreeMap::new(),
            total: self.total,
        };
        for key in layer.changed() {
            if let Some((owner, bytes)) = self.owners.get(&key) {
                plan.total.remove(*bytes);
                plan.principals
                    .entry(owner.clone())
                    .or_insert(self.principals[owner])
                    .remove(*bytes);
            }
            let owner = if layer.eph.get(&key.0, &key.1)?.is_some() {
                let size = declared_size(&schema.tables[&key.0]);
                plan.total.add(size);
                plan.principals
                    .entry(viewer.into())
                    .or_insert_with(|| self.principals.get(viewer).copied().unwrap_or_default())
                    .add(size);
                Some((viewer.into(), size))
            } else {
                None
            };
            plan.changes.push((key, owner));
        }
        if plan.total.rows > limits.rows
            || plan.total.bytes > limits.bytes
            || plan
                .principals
                .values()
                .any(|c| c.rows > limits.principal_rows || c.bytes > limits.principal_bytes)
        {
            return Err(full());
        }
        Ok(plan)
    }
    fn apply(&mut self, plan: Plan) {
        for (key, owner) in plan.changes {
            match owner {
                Some(owner) => {
                    self.owners.insert(key, owner);
                }
                None => {
                    self.owners.remove(&key);
                }
            }
        }
        for (owner, count) in plan.principals {
            if count.rows == 0 {
                self.principals.remove(&owner);
            } else {
                self.principals.insert(owner, count);
snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/fork.rs:5:    pub fn fork_memory(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {

//! Isolated complete fixtures, including captured authority and asset ownership.
use super::*;
impl Store {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#122-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — fork committed state, never reinterpret stored rows as new seed input.
    pub fn fork_memory(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let mut fork = Self::memory()?;
        {
            let backup = rusqlite::backup::Backup::new(&self.conn, &mut fork.conn)?;
            backup.run_to_completion(256, std::time::Duration::ZERO, None)?;
        }
        fork.catalog = self.catalog.clone();
        fork.identity = self.identity.clone();
        fork.server = self.server;
        if self.assets_dir.exists() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(&fork.assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&self.assets_dir).map_err(io)? {
                let entry = entry.map_err(io)?;
                if !entry.file_type().map_err(io)?.is_file() {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset ledger contains a non-file".into()));
                }
                std::fs::copy(entry.path(), fork.assets_dir.join(entry.file_name())).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(fork)
    }
}

fn io(e:std::io::Error)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string())}

impl Store {
    /// Independent valid worlds share verified bytes, never source row state.
    pub fn empty_with_asset_ledger(&self)->Result<Self,RuntimeError>{
        let fresh=Self::memory()?;
        let ids=self.conn.prepare("SELECT id FROM _assets")?.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
        for id in ids{
            let mut asset=self.asset(&id)?.ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset disappeared".into()))?;
            asset.home=None;asset.released_at=None;
            fresh.record_asset(&asset)?;
        }
        if self.assets_dir.exists(){
            std::fs::create_dir_all(&fresh.assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&self.assets_dir).map_err(io)?{
                let entry=entry.map_err(io)?;
                if !entry.file_type().map_err(io)?.is_file(){return Err(RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset is not a regular file".into()));}
                std::fs::copy(entry.path(),fresh.assets_dir.join(entry.file_name())).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(fresh)
    }
}
fn run_pair(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,row:&Value,fixture:Option<&Engine>,trace:Option<(&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,&[histories::Step])>,out:&mut Campaign){
    run_pair_variant(bound,inv,rows,row,fixture,trace,false,out);
    if inv.column.as_ref().and_then(|c|bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(c)).is_some_and(C::is_optional){run_pair_variant(bound,inv,rows,row,fixture,trace,true,out);}
}
pub(super) fn generated(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>){
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id")&&sites.is_none_or(|s|s.contains(&i.site.to_string()))){
        if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(super::super::temporal::has_clock){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: column clock pairs use the campaign clock only; other temporal ticks not covered",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap()));}
        for variant in 0..16{
            if !reserve(out,1){return;}
            let rows=match snapshot(bound,inv,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column snapshot: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            let e=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$column.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column seed: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            let Ok(rows)=readback(&e)else{continue;};
            for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){if out.exhausted(){return;}run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,None,None,out);}
            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()){break;}
        }
    }
}
pub(super) fn fixture(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    let Ok(rows)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id")){
        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){if out.exhausted(){return;}run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,Some(fixture),None,out);}
    }
}

//! Exercise actual logical lifetime reads between deadlines and cleanup ticks.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#102-temporal-intent — no snapshot-only clock refutation.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>){
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_none()&&i.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(super::super::temporal::has_clock)
        && sites.is_none_or(|s|s.contains(&i.site.to_string()))) {
        let policy=inv.policy.as_ref().unwrap();
        if policy.has_reference(){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: temporal reference policy needs authorized per-tick engine replay",inv.table));continue;}
        if !kernel::dependencies(policy).is_empty(){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: clock policy with raw-probe dependencies needs a live-snapshot evaluator",inv.table));continue;}
        let name="$security.temporal";
        let probe=program(name,ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(Read{site:"temporal security".into(),table:inv.table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),
            key:vec![Expr::Literal(id(&inv.table))],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed}))]);
        for variant in [0,4] {
            if !out.reserve(1){return;}
            let rows=match snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,true){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            for now in [NOW,NOW+999,NOW+1000,NOW+1001,NOW+1999,NOW+2000,NOW+2001] {
                if !out.reserve(1){return;}
                // Recreate the store for each boundary; never sweep before the
                // read. Unswept expiry remains a logical absence by contract.
                let mut engine=match engine(bound,&rows,probe.clone()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                let actual=match readback(&engine){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                let Some(row)=actual.get(&inv.table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())else{continue;};
                for viewer in [VIEWER,OTHER] {
                    if !out.reserve(1){return;}
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    let Ok(q)=engine.query(name,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(now))else{continue;};
                    if q.unavailable||!q.complete||q.data==Value::Null{continue;}
                    // Scalar clock atoms are evaluated only after a real
                    // engine read returned this committed row. This extra
                    // field is proof-private and never enters the store.
                    let mut image=row.clone();
                    if let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image {fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(now));}
                    if matches!(rules::evaluate(policy,viewer,None,&image,false,&mut Snapshot{schema:&bound.schema,rows:&actual}),Ok(false)) {
                        out.admitted+=1;
                        out.violations.push(Counterexample {reachability:Reachability::SyntheticOperation,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:name.into(),
                            effect:"read".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:actual.clone(),rule_path:format!("server read at {now}, before any physical sweep; clock policy false"),
                            scope:"actual engine read over schema-valid committed seed ingress at the recorded server clock; schedules are not assumed to have run".into()});
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
    }}}
    out
}
pub(super) fn fixture_cases(p:&Program,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],out:&mut Campaign)->Vec<BTreeMap<String,Value>>{
    let args=&p.args;
    let mut literals=BTreeMap::<String,Vec<Value>>::new();
    fn collect(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>){
        if let Some(binary)=value.get("Binary") {for (a,b) in [("left","right"),("right","left")] {
            if let (Some(name),Some(literal))=(binary.get(a).and_then(|v|v.get("Variable")).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str),binary.get(b).and_then(|v|v.get("Literal"))) {
                if let Ok(value)=serde_json::from_value::<Value>(literal.clone()){out.entry(name.into()).or_default().push(value);}
            }
        }}
        match value{serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>for v in xs.values(){collect(v,out)},serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for v in xs{collect(v,out)},_=>{}}
    }
    collect(&serde_json::to_value(p).unwrap(),&mut literals);
    fn values(name:&str,k:&ir::ArgType,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String])->Option<Vec<Value>>{
        Some(match k{
            ir::ArgType::Enum{variants}=>variants.iter().map(|s|Value::String(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Text{min,max,..}=>['z','y'].into_iter().map(|c|Value::String(c.to_string().repeat((*min).max(1).min(*max) as usize))).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Int=>vec![Value::Int(0),Value::Int(1),Value::Int(2)],
            ir::ArgType::IntRange{min,max}=>{let mut xs=vec![*min,1i64.clamp(*min,*max),*max];xs.sort();xs.dedup();xs.into_iter().map(Value::Int).collect()},
            ir::ArgType::Bool=>vec![Value::Bool(false),Value::Bool(true)],
            ir::ArgType::Time=>vec![Value::Int(NOW-86_400_000),Value::Int(NOW),Value::Int(NOW+86_400_000)],
            ir::ArgType::Principal=>actors.iter().map(|s|Value::Id(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Ref{table} if rows.get(table).is_some_and(|rs|!rs.is_empty())=>rows[table].iter().filter_map(|r|r.field("id").ok().cloned()).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Optional(inner)=>{let mut v=vec![Value::Null];v.extend(values(name,inner,rows,actors)?);v},
            _=>vec![arguments(&BTreeMap::from([(name.into(),k.clone())]),0)?.remove(name)?],
        })
    }
    let mut cases=vec![BTreeMap::new()];
    for (name,k) in args{
        let Some(mut vals)=values(name,k,rows,actors)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{name}: history needs a typed asset argument"));return Vec::new();};
        for literal in literals.get(name).into_iter().flatten() {
            let valid=match (k,literal){
                (ir::ArgType::Text{min,max,..},Value::String(s))=>s.chars().filter(|c|!c.is_whitespace()).count()>=*min as usize&&s.chars().count()<=*max as usize,
                (ir::ArgType::Int,Value::Int(_))=>true,
                (ir::ArgType::IntRange{min,max},Value::Int(n))=>(*min..=*max).contains(n),_=>false,
            };
            if valid&&!vals.contains(literal){vals.insert(0,literal.clone());}
        }
        let mut next=Vec::new();
        'product:for offset in 0..vals.len(){for n in 0..cases.len().max(vals.len()){
            if out.exhausted(){return next;}
            let mut case=cases[n%cases.len()].clone();case.insert(name.clone(),vals[(n+offset)%vals.len()].clone());
            if !next.contains(&case){next.push(case);}if next.len()==LIMIT{out.unsupported.insert("history arguments capped at 512 combinations".into());break 'product;}
        }}
        cases=next;
    }cases
}
pub(super) fn trace_key(steps:&[Step])->Vec<(&str,&str)>{steps.iter().map(|s|(s.operation.as_str(),s.viewer.as_str())).collect()}
fn violation(out:&mut Campaign,inv:&Invariant,steps:&[Step],viewer:&str,row:&Value,old:Option<&Value>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,effect:&str)->bool{
                expected_release: previous,
                expected_revision: revision,
                digest,
                rollback,
                restore_dormant,
                allow_feed_adoption,
            },
            |pending| { if let Some(candidate)=candidate.as_deref_mut() {candidate.pending=pending;} },
        )?;
        if options.json {println!("{}",result);} else {
            println!("release {release} is current as generation {}; {}; running serve adopted the release",result["generation"],result["adoption"].as_str().unwrap_or("adopted"));
            for fill in result["backfills"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(|f|f.as_str()) {println!("  backfill: {fill}");}
            if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {println!("{}",result["details"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
    // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
    // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
    let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
    match result {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(error) => {
            eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
            1
        }
    }
}

/// The store in memory: rows by (table, id) and index entries by their key
/// bytes, the server's layout without SQLite. For tests of the device path
/// and for a host that keeps a partition on the heap.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct MemoryRows {
    sequences: std::cell::RefCell<std::collections::BTreeMap<(String, String), i64>>,
    rows: std::cell::RefCell<std::collections::BTreeMap<(String, String), Value>>,
    entries: std::cell::RefCell<std::collections::BTreeMap<(String, Vec<u8>), String>>,
}

impl MemoryRows {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#137-wave-19-correction-admissible-starts-and-persistent-refutations — isolated campaign hubs copy rows and index/sequence state together.
    pub(crate) fn fork_fixture(&self)->Self {
        Self{sequences:self.sequences.clone(),rows:self.rows.clone(),entries:self.entries.clone()}
    }
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    /// Every row of a table, by id.
    pub fn rows(&self, table: &str) -> Vec<Value> {
        self.rows.borrow().iter().filter(|((t, _), _)| t == table).map(|(_, row)| row.clone()).collect()
    }

    fn range(&self, table: &str, index_name: &str, lo: &[u8], hi: &[u8], dir: Dir, limit: usize) -> Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)> {
        let ti = format!("{table}.{index_name}");
        let entries = self.entries.borrow();
        let rows = self.rows.borrow();
        let within = entries.range((ti.clone(), lo.to_vec())..(ti, hi.to_vec()));

### 4.4 Server (`crates/snapback4`)

- `serve.rs` `/m/` (930-1087):
  1. Before the replay lookup (947), an ephemeral operation checks the rate
     bucket.
  2. It takes a pooled reader (as `/q/` does at 995-1015), with the same
     in-memory fallback to the serving engine under `--fresh`
     (`serve.rs:1010-1013`), and calls `mutate_ephemeral`.
  3. It replies HTTP 200 with
     `{ "state": "sent", "id": <id|null>, "result": <json>, "ephemeral": { "epoch", "incarnation", "tables" }, "store_id", "head" }`
     and no `seq`. A refusal replies HTTP 200
     `{ "state": "failed", "id", "why", "store_id", "head" }`.
- `/q/` answers of queries with `ephemeralReads` carry
  `"ephemeral": { "epoch", "incarnation" }` (§3.3).
- `Shared.wake` and `polls` (§3.3). The durable commit path at 1075-1079
  updates `wake.seq` and notifies `polls` as well as `committed`.
- `serve_changes.rs` (29-55):
  - §3.3's parameters, snapshot-under-mutex waiting, wake filtering,
    100 ms pacing and the answer field;
  - `presence_reconnected`'s read-first check.
- `serve_maintenance.rs` (116-137): the hub sweep on every pass.
- `serve_adoption.rs` (157-190) and engine replacement: hub
  `clear_and_rebind` under the engine mutex.
- `verbs.rs`: `data` and `sql` skip ephemeral tables and say why.

### 4.5 Device (`crates/snapback4-device`)

- `admit_with_replay` (`device.rs:952`) refuses an ephemeral operation with
  `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, as a guard. The client never sends one there.
- Reads of an ephemeral table are already `unknown`, because the table has
  no `sync` (`require_acquired`, `device.rs:320-333`).
- The wasm budget (`metrics/wasm-budget.json`: 578,207 B, against 577,207 B
  measured on the black M5 and 576,963 B on the mini): if the device's wasm
  exceeds it, the lane raises the budget in the same commit and records
  the reason.

### 4.6 Client (`packages/snapback4`)

- `contract.ts`: `Mutation`/`Ephemeral`/`Signal` as in §2.4, and the
  `mutate` overloads. `backend.ts`: the new operation facts.
- A new `src/ephemeral.ts` implements the lane (§2.4):
  - order;
  - coalescing and the window for `renews` operations;
  - offline;
  - the 256 bound;
  - refusals that do not change the link;
  - `POST /m/<name>` with a correlation id and no `MutationIds`;
  - no `onWrite` or outbox bookkeeping.
- Branch on `op.ephemeral` at the top of every `mutate`:
  - `client.ts` (658-685);
  - `local.ts` (1152-1205);
  - `app-online.ts` (105-150);
  - `app-client.ts` (82-106);
  - `app.ts` (201, 346);
  - `mock.ts`, where the labelled mock keeps ephemeral rows in memory with
    the same life semantics.
- Interest and polling:
  - `changes.ts` (48): the three parameters.
  - Both poll loops (`client.ts:505-537` and `local.ts:806-845`):
    - keep the interest set from `ephemeralReads`;
    - abort and re-issue on growth, with §3.3's `eph`;
    - re-run intersecting watches on an answer's `ephemeral.tables`,
      without counting it as a change and without `/sync`;
    - re-run every ephemeral watch on an incarnation change;
    - keep polling while any watch reads an ephemeral table.
- Fencing (§3.3): the per-watch `(incarnation, epoch)` floor, in
  `client.ts` `currentRead` (413) and in the `local-query.ts` publication
  path.
- `react.tsx`: `useMutation`'s `run` follows the overloads; `usePendingWrite`
  takes only `Mutation`.

### 4.7 Tests
### 4.7 Tests

Each of the following becomes a test the suite runs.

**Language** (`crates/snapback4-lang/tests`):
- Every refusal in §2.1 and §2.2, with its code, message and fix text:
  - a rule on an ephemeral table probing another ephemeral table;
  - a mixed write hidden inside an expression block (IR probe);
  - a mixed write through a helper.
- The classification, `renews` (positive and negative cases, including the
  `n: old.n + 1` upsert keyed by `viewer`, a toggle and a returning
  operation) and `ephemeralReads`.
- `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`.
- The emitted facts.
- A schema with no ephemeral table emits and serializes byte-identically,
  compared with fixtures captured at the base commit before any change.

**Guide:** the `ephemeral` card compiles and runs:
- typing set, read by a peer, expired at `life`;
- the chats-list query;
- viewing presence.

**Core:**
- `Layered` routing.
- Durable-write refusal in ephemeral mode.
- The drop-guard undo on a refusal after a successful earlier write, for
  each of these: a unique clash, a cap, a rule, a store error.
- The renewal law: a member removed at t=1100 is refused at the next
  renewal, and their row is gone by t = 1100 + life. This is the reviewers'
  probe, inverted.
- Joins from an ephemeral table to durable tables under their rules.
- Expiry before rules.
- `$deadline` absent from point reads, scans and mutation-returned rows.
- Per-principal and global capacity refusals: the row is absent and the
  epoch unmoved.
- A **paired-world capacity test**: two worlds with equal topology and
  different private payload lengths give identical admission.
- The generation fence: a reader whose transaction predates adoption is
  refused, and the hub is rebound.
- Nothing is written to `_commits`, `_log`, `_outbox` or `_ops` (assert
  row counts before and after).
- Wake order: a publisher paused between its commit and its wake
  publication, across another commit and across adoption, never leaves the
  wake state behind the hub's epoch or incarnation.

**Server** (a new `tests/serve_ephemeral` module beside `serve_presence`):
- An ephemeral mutation succeeds while another connection holds a SQLite
  write transaction.
- A peer's poll **already blocked inside `wait_timeout`** with
  `ephTables=typing` answers within 250 ms of a `setTyping`, and names
  `typing`. This is the lost-wakeup test.
- A poll with `ephTables=viewing` does not answer on a write to `typing`.
- A poll with no `eph` parameters on a schema with no ephemeral tables
  answers byte-identically to the base.
- The 100 ms pacing.
- The rate refusal, as HTTP 200 `failed`.
- Restart and adoption change the incarnation.
- A removed member's next read is empty.

**Client:**
- The lane:
  - order: a delayed `set → set → stop` settles as stopped;
  - `renews` coalescing, where the replaced promise resolves with its
    replacement's outcome;
  - non-`renews` calls are never coalesced or suppressed;
  - the renewal window at call time and at dequeue, including that an
    intervening `stopTyping` closes it;
  - the 256 bound.
- An offline call, and every call waiting when the link drops, resolves at
  once with `E_OFFLINE`, and leaves nothing in the outbox or journal.
- A capacity or rate refusal leaves the link `online`.
- A poll already held before a watch with `ephemeralReads` mounts is
  re-issued, and the watch sees a later write within 500 ms. This is run on
  the local-first tier, which is the benchmark's tier, and on the online
  tier.
- The `(incarnation, epoch)` fencing and the ephemeral revision:
  - a stale same-incarnation answer is dropped;
  - a new-incarnation epoch-1 answer is accepted;
  - delayed `/q/` and `/changes` responses from the retired incarnation,
    arriving after the replacement, are dropped.
- An incomplete ephemeral answer is re-fetched at the 1 s cadence, and picks
  up a row made reachable by silent reclamation, without either
  notification counter changing.
- The `nextExpiry` re-fetch.
- In the local-first tier, an ephemeral invalidation re-runs the watch
  without `/sync`.
- Type tests:
  - `mutate(api.eph)` is `Signal<R>`;
  - widening `Ephemeral` to `Mutation` is a type error;
  - `usePendingWrite(api.eph)` is a type error;
  - the card's React snippet compiles.

**End-to-end** (`packages/snapback4/test/ephemeral-e2e.test.mjs`, a real CLI
server, two clients on the local-first tier). This is the acceptance test:
1. Client B mounts `typingIn` after its poll is already held.
2. Client A calls `setTyping` 45 times at 25 ms intervals, as the benchmark
   does.
3. The test records when B first sees A and when B stops seeing A.
4. Over five trials:
   - first-seen must be at most 500 ms after A's first call;
   - last-seen must be at most `life + 500 ms` after A's last call;
   - with `ephemeral 4s`.
5. A durable-typing control (the old recipe) runs beside it. Its numbers
   are recorded, not asserted, so the change is measured, not assumed.

**Suite:** `scripts/test.sh` stays green, and the wasm build stays within
budget.

### 4.8 Sequence and publication

1. Revision 1 landed on main as Draft. It then had three blind reviews
   (§8): Astra `gpt-6-astra` at max effort, Grok 4.7 at xhigh, and Opus 5.5
   at max effort.
2. Revision 2 folds all three reviews. Astra, the family that was NOT
   READY, gets one bounded delta check of revision 2, which runs in
   parallel with lane E1. Anything it finds is folded into the lanes by a
   continuation brief. There is no further design round.
3. E1, then E2 from E1's server checkpoint.
4. Two blind code reviews of the integrated branch, by Astra and Grok, with
   at most two fix rounds.
5. Land on main.
6. Publish the next free `snapback4` version with `scripts/publish.mjs`,
   plus `snapback4-expo`.
7. Run a Clique calibration with Astra medium on the published pin, and
   record it under `benchmarks/runs/`.

## 5. How we will know

and adds update-only column intent. Check has a compact note block and bounded
concrete work; test/audit retain full campaigns. Source evidence is read-only;
per-lane measurements and qualification are in reports/20261019-sec4.
DX4 owns staging/upload/dev papercuts; QRY owns read return and hidden lookups.

## 2026-10-19 — SEC4 wave-19 correction

Merged origin/main before changes. Coordinator wave-19 addendum supersedes the
seed-only demotion: authored fixture read/integrity violations fail. Empty
fixtures generate; payload initialization executes authored creators, preserving
requires, principal-role checks and copied values. Exact-source/fixture/CLI

and rule-changing deployment prints a matching-campaign demand. Real role and

and wave-19 evidence/report record reviews, qualification and remaining gaps.
Column read rules remain outside SEC4; release visibility belongs to its owner.

and grantRole → read paths. Counterfactual column mutation must not break an
immutable creator copy; actual typed payloads remain observable. Independent
review reproduced source-change-during-test and followed-symlink fingerprint
bugs. Tests now consume captured source strings and private fixture snapshots,

The exact social starter also has a native campaign regression.
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).

   129	pub(super) fn role_values(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str)->Vec<String>{
   130	    let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(roster)else{return Vec::new();};
   131	    let Some(kind)=table.columns.get(column)else{return Vec::new();};

   136	            RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles),_=>{}
   137	        }}

    82	// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#137-wave-19-correction-admissible-starts-and-persistent-refutations — creator guards/copies are part of generated initialization.
    83	pub fn fixture(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign)->Result<Engine,String>{
    84	    let proposed=proposals(bound,out)?;
    85	    let rows=readback(&proposed)?;
    86	    let actors=histories::actors(bound,&rows);
    87	    let rosters=bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,_)|!super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,name).is_empty()).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    88	    let creators=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!p.program.native&&!bound.schema.operations.get(&p.program.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral)&&ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(_,kind)|matches!(*kind,"insert"|"upsert"))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    89	    let created=creators.iter().flat_map(|p|ir::write_tables(&p.program)).filter(|(_,kind)|matches!(*kind,"insert"|"upsert")).map(|(t,_)|t).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    90	    let mut engine=engine_on_store(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new(),proposed.store().empty_with_asset_ledger().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?)?;
    91	    let mut pending=rows.iter().filter(|(t,_)|rosters.contains(*t)||!created.contains(*t)).flat_map(|(t,rs)|rs.iter().map(move|r|(t.clone(),r.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    92	    // Bootstrap proposals require an actor admitted by the insert rule in the
    93	    // previous world. Never let a proposed role authorize its own insertion.
    94	    for _ in 0..=bound.schema.tables.len(){
    95	        let before=pending.len();let mut retry=Vec::new();
    96	        for (table,row) in pending{
    97	            if !out.reserve(1){out.unsupported.insert("generated initialization work exhausted; remaining populations uncovered".into());validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;return Ok(engine);}
    98	            let gate=&bound.schema.tables[&table].rules.insert;
    99	            if !actors.iter().any(|actor|matches!(engine.fixture_policy(gate,actor,None,&row,NOW),Ok(rules::Verdict::Readable))) {retry.push((table,row));continue;}
   100	            let mut candidate=engine.fork_fixture().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
   101	            let seeded=clean_row(bound,&table,&row);
   102	            if candidate.seed_at([(table.clone(),seeded)],NOW).is_ok(){engine=candidate;}else{retry.push((table,row));}
   103	        }
   104	        pending=retry;if pending.len()==before{break;}
   105	    }
   106	    for (table,_) in pending{out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}: no admitted insert/bootstrap; population uncovered"));}
   107	    // Authored insertion bodies preserve requires, immutable copies, defaults,
