### 2.4 The client

**Generated facts.** For an ephemeral mutation, the generated operation
carries:
- `ephemeral: true`;
- `renews: true` when the binder marked it;
- `renewMs`: a quarter of the shortest life among the tables it writes, in
  milliseconds.

For every query, the generated operation carries `ephemeralReads`: the
static list of ephemeral tables its read closure can touch (read sites,
helpers, and native reads treated conservatively), omitted when empty.

The same facts appear on the operation entries of `/schema`.

```ts
export const setTyping: Ephemeral<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, null> =
  { kind: "mutation", name: "setTyping", runs: "server", predicts: "never", ephemeral: true, renews: true, renewMs: 1000 };
export const typingIn: Query<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, { principal: Principal }[]> =
  { kind: "query", name: "typingIn", runs: "server", ephemeralReads: ["typing"] };
```

**Types.** Durable and ephemeral mutations are disjoint types over a common
base, so widening cannot pick the wrong overload:

```ts
export type Mutation<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral?: false };
export type Ephemeral<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral: true;
  readonly renews?: true; readonly renewMs: number };
export type Signal<R = unknown> =
  | { readonly state: "sent"; readonly id: string; readonly result: R }
  | { readonly state: "failed"; readonly id: string; readonly why: Refusal };
```

`client.mutate(op, args)` resolves to `Signal<R>` for an `Ephemeral` and to
`Write<R>` for a `Mutation`. It takes no `MutateOptions` for an ephemeral
operation. `useMutation(op)` keeps its `{ run, last, inFlight }` shape and
types `run`'s result the same way. `usePendingWrite` accepts only
`Mutation`. Negative type tests pin all of this.

**Transport.** Calls travel the ordinary `POST /m/<name>` route, but they
bypass everything durable:

- the device outbox and its admission journal;
- the online tier's pending queue;
- `app-online.ts`'s durable journal;
- the browser admission path in `app-client.ts`;
- prediction.

**The ephemeral lane.** Each client has one ephemeral lane. It keeps at most
one request in flight and sends calls in call order. Keeping calls in order
means a `stopTyping` can never be overtaken by an earlier `setTyping`.

1. **Coalescing, `renews` operations only.**
   - The key is the operation name plus the canonical JSON of its arguments.
   - A new `renews` call whose key matches a call still waiting in the lane
     removes that waiting call and joins the end of the lane. The removed
     call's promise resolves with the new call's outcome.
   - A call that is already in flight is never replaced.
   - Non-`renews` operations are never coalesced.
2. **Renewal window, `renews` operations only.**
   - A call is not sent, and resolves at once with the previous result, if
     all of these hold:
     - its key equals that of the lane's last successfully sent call;
     - that call was sent less than `renewMs` ago;
     - the lane has sent no other call since, and none is waiting.
   - The rule is applied both when the call is made and when it reaches the
     head of the lane.
   - The skipped call's row is still alive for at least three quarters of
     its life.
   - A keystroke-per-call typing indicator therefore sends about once per
     `renewMs`, and its row expires between `0.75 × life` and `life` after
     the last keystroke.
3. **Offline.**
   - While the client's link is not `online`, a call resolves at once with
     `{ state: "failed", why: E_OFFLINE }`. It is never queued or retried.
   - When the link goes offline, every waiting call fails the same way.
   - `E_OFFLINE`'s card text gains that sentence: an ephemeral write is
     dropped, not held.
4. **Bounded.**
   - The lane holds at most 256 waiting calls.
   - Beyond that, a new call fails locally with `E_RATE_LIMIT` and is not
     sent.
5. **Refusals do not change the link.**
   - Engine refusals arrive as HTTP 200 with `{ state: "failed", why }`:
     rule, capacity, rate, generation. HTTP admission may instead return
     `{ denied }` with 503; the client validates and preserves that refusal
     without replaying the call or dropping waiting calls.
   - The lane changes the client's link state only on a transport failure:
     no response, or a response that is not this route's JSON.

**Watches need no new API.**
- A query with `ephemeralReads` joins the client's ephemeral interest set
  when it is observed, before its first answer.
- When that set grows, the held `/changes` request is aborted and re-issued
  at once. Shrinking never restarts it.
- A watch is re-run when a `/changes` answer names one of its ephemeral
  tables, and at its `nextExpiry`.
- In the local-first tier, this re-run never triggers a `/sync`, because
  ephemeral changes are not commits.
- §3.3 has the exact parameters and the fencing.

**Expiry recovery (2026-09-27, lane FK).** A local watch can receive a
server answer after its `nextExpiry`, including when the client's estimate
reaches that deadline just before the server does. Withhold that answer and
retry through the ordinary query/publication fences. Keep one request in
flight per watch; repeated expired replies back off at 25, 50, 100 ms and
so on, capped at 1 s, until a current answer arrives or the watch ends.
Without a new wakeup the first expired reply left the watch loading forever;
deadline expiry changes neither the durable sequence nor the ephemeral epoch.
`ephemeral-e2e.test.mjs` now forces the early estimate and keeps its original
empty-result and timing assertions; `expired-server-watch.test.mjs` covers
pacing, withholding, recovery and teardown, with and without `keep`.

The recipes the cards will teach:

```tsx
const typing = useQuery(api.typingIn, { roomId });
const setTyping = useMutation(api.setTyping);
const stopTyping = useMutation(api.stopTyping);
<Composer onChangeText={text => text ? setTyping.run({ roomId }) : stopTyping.run({ roomId })}
          onSend={() => stopTyping.run({ roomId })} />
<TypingIndicator visible={"data" in typing && (typing.data?.length ?? 0) > 0} />
```

The lane compiles this snippet against the real `Read<R>` union and adjusts
the narrowing to whatever the union requires. The card shows the compiled
form.

For presence in a scope ("who has this chat open"), declare
`table viewing … ephemeral 30s` with a `renews` `markViewing` operation. The
card's snippet calls it on mount and every 10 s, and calls a delete
operation on unmount.

### 2.5 Documentation
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

                    lines.push(format!("  {context}"));
                    shown_context.push((note.code.clone(), context.clone()));
                }
            }
            shown_compacts.push(parts.clone());
            shown_codes.push(note.code.clone());
            continue;
        }
        if shown_codes.contains(&note.code) {
            // A repeat that carries a form not printed yet keeps its own line, then the form.
            lines.push(format!("note: {} — {}: (as above) {}{times}", note.site, note.code, short(&note.summary)));
        } else {
            let below = note.form.as_ref().is_some_and(|(_, code)| !shown_forms.contains(code));
            let summary = match note.summary.strip_suffix(" (check --verbose)") { Some(text) if below => format!("{text} (below)"), _ => note.summary.clone() };
            lines.push(format!("note: {} — {}: {}. {summary}{times}", note.site, note.code, verdict(&note.code, &note.summary, below)));
            shown_codes.push(note.code.clone());
        }
        if let Some((lead, code)) = &note.form && !shown_forms.contains(code) {
            lines.push(format!("  rewrite: {lead}"));
            lines.extend(code.lines().map(|line| format!("    {line}")));
            shown_forms.push(code.clone());
        }
    }
    flush(&mut lines, &mut repeats);
    // Every advisory line has a direct route to the acceptance recipe.
    for line in &mut lines {
        if let Some(code) = line.split_whitespace().find(|word| word.starts_with("N_")) {
            let code = code.trim_end_matches(':').to_owned();
            line.push_str(&format!(" — snapback4 why {code}"));
        }
    }
    lines
}

/// The operation or table a repeat is about (`query b`), else its short subject.
fn subject(summary: &str) -> String {
    let mut words = summary.splitn(3, ' ');
    match (words.next(), words.next()) {
        (Some(kind @ ("query" | "mutation" | "job" | "action" | "table" | "view")), Some(name)) => format!("{kind} {}", name.trim_end_matches(':')),
        _ => short(summary),
    }
}

/// The subject of a repeated note: its first clause before any quoted source, at most 100 characters.
fn short(summary: &str) -> String {
    let clause = summary.split("; ").next().unwrap_or(summary);
    // The quoted source is above already; the subject before it names the repeat.
    let clause = clause.split_once(" `").map_or(clause, |(subject, _)| subject).trim_end_matches(':');
    match clause.char_indices().nth(100) {
        Some((at, _)) => format!("{}…", &clause[..at]),
        None => clause.to_owned(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn note(site: &str, code: &str, summary: &str) -> Note { Note { site: site.into(), code: code.into(), summary: summary.into(), form: None, compact: None } }

    #[test]
    fn a_compiled_complete_form_prints_once_per_lead_by_default() {
        let rewrite = "Branch on the grant and pin the owner for everyone else; both pages are complete:\nquery q(c: cursor ?):\n  if exists staff[viewer]:\n    return t last 50 by .at after c\n  return t where .owner = viewer last 50 by .at after c\n\nOtherwise: pin .owner";
        let form = compiled_form("N_NEVER_COMPLETE", rewrite);
        assert_eq!(form.as_ref().map(|(lead, _)| lead.as_str()), Some("Branch on the grant and pin the owner for everyone else; both pages are complete:"));
        assert!(compiled_form("N_NEVER_COMPLETE", "pin .owner with where equalities").is_none());
        assert!(compiled_form("N_SYNC_ORDER", rewrite).is_none());
        let with = |site: &str| Note { form: form.clone(), ..note(site, "N_NEVER_COMPLETE", "query q: never complete") };
        let lines = render(vec![with("schema.q:1:1"), with("schema.q:9:1")]);
        assert_eq!(lines[2..8], [
            "note: schema.q:1:1 — N_NEVER_COMPLETE: fix: use the complete form below, or accept `complete: false`. query q: never complete — snapback4 why N_NEVER_COMPLETE".to_owned(),
            "  rewrite: Branch on the grant and pin the owner for everyone else; both pages are complete:".into(),
            "    query q(c: cursor ?):".into(),
            "      if exists staff[viewer]:".into(),
            "        return t last 50 by .at after c".into(),
            "      return t where .owner = viewer last 50 by .at after c".into(),
        ], "{lines:#?}");
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 9, "the second note of the same form is the short repeat: {lines:#?}");
        assert_eq!(lines[8], "note: N_NEVER_COMPLETE (as above) also for query q (schema.q:9:1) — snapback4 why N_NEVER_COMPLETE", "{lines:#?}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn distinct_scans_at_one_site_and_distinct_query_repairs_remain_visible() {
        let n = |subject: &str, repair: &str| Note { compact: Some([repair.into(), "impact".into(), "audience".into()]), ..note("s.q:1:1", "N_NEVER_COMPLETE", subject) };
        let lines = render(vec![n("query q: scan a", "rewrite a"), n("query q: scan b", "rewrite b"), n("query r", "rewrite r"), n("query r", "rewrite r")]);
        let output = lines.join("\n");
        for repair in ["rewrite a", "rewrite b", "rewrite r"] { assert_eq!(output.matches(repair).count(), 1, "{output}"); }
        assert!(output.contains("×2 at this site"), "{output}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn identical_actions_keep_different_scan_audiences() {
        let with = |audience: &str| Note { compact: Some(["review query both: missing pin .owner".into(), "same impact".into(), audience.into()]), ..note("s.q:1:1", "N_NEVER_COMPLETE", "query both") };
    })}
    args.iter().map(|(n,k)|one(k,variant).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
}

fn materialize_arguments(engine:&mut Engine,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,values:&BTreeMap<String,Value>,viewer:&str)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Value>,String>{
    fn one(e:&mut Engine,k:&ir::ArgType,v:&Value,viewer:&str)->Result<Value,String>{Ok(match (k,v){
        (ir::ArgType::Optional(inner),v) if *v!=Value::Null=>one(e,inner,v,viewer)?,
        (ir::ArgType::List{of:Some(inner),..}|ir::ArgType::ListRange{of:Some(inner),..},Value::Array(xs))=>Value::Array(xs.iter().map(|v|one(e,inner,v,viewer)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        (ir::ArgType::Image{..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:image"=>e.store_asset(viewer,&PNG,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.column_value(),
        (ir::ArgType::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(e,viewer,types)?,
        _=>v.clone(),
    })}
    values.iter().map(|(n,v)|one(engine,&args[n],v,viewer).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
}

/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
    for (name,_) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_some()){campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("ephemeral {name}: generated memory populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain uncovered; durable campaigns use isolated hub copies"));}
    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
}
/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if !campaign.generated_fixture {trusted_reads(bound,fixture,campaign);}
    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
    }}
}
pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    fixture_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    let fixtures=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());
    if fixtures.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{campaign.unsupported.insert("operation fixture matrix exceeds 1000 rows; provide a focused security fixture".into());return;}
    let mut viewers=histories::viewers(bound);
    for (table,rows) in &fixtures{for row in rows{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!viewers.contains(p){viewers.push(p.clone());}}}}
    let eligible=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)&&!p.program.native).count();
    let quota=(8192/eligible.max(1)).clamp(1,512);let mut remaining=8192usize;
    for p in &bound.programs{
        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}
        if ephemeral_operation(bound,&p.program,campaign){continue;}
        let target=bound.schema.visible_tables().find(|(t,_)|super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t)).or_else(||bound.schema.visible_tables().next()).map(|(t,_)|t.clone());
        let Some(target)=target else{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no table population for generated arguments",p.program.name));continue;};
        let dummy=Invariant{table:target,target:"read".into(),column:None,predicate:"allow".into(),site:crate::Site::default(),policy:Some(RuleExpr::Allow),operation:Some(p.program.name.clone()),requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None};
        let cases=histories::fixture_cases(&p.program,&fixtures,&viewers,campaign);
        let mut exercised=0;
        'cases:for (variant,mut args) in cases.into_iter().enumerate(){
            let rows=if has_fixture{fixtures.clone()}else{match snapshot(bound,&dummy,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));break;}}};
            if !has_fixture{for (arg,kind) in &p.program.args {if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind&&let Some(row)=rows.get(table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())&&let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(arg.clone(),id.clone());}}}
            for viewer in &viewers{
                if exercised==quota||remaining==0{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: operation matrix capped at {quota} calls to share the 8192-call budget across every operation; remaining arguments/viewers not covered",p.program.name));break 'cases;}
                exercised+=1;remaining-=1;
                let mut engine=match if has_fixture {fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())}else{engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.matrix",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))}{Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut engine,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                campaign.attempted+=1;*campaign.operations.entry(p.program.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                if p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query{
                    match engine.query(&p.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(_)=>{},Err(e) if e.code=="E_INPUT"=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated arguments rejected as E_INPUT",p.program.name));},Err(_)=>campaign.refused+=1,}
                }else{
                    match engine.mutate(&p.program.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>campaign.refused+=1,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: matrix engine error {e}",p.program.name));},_=>{},}
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

fn ephemeral_operation(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,out:&mut Campaign)->bool{
    let unsupported=p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&bound.schema.operations.get(&p.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral);
    if unsupported{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: ephemeral mutation history is not covered by the durable campaign",p.name));}
    unsupported
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="always"){
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
            if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            // A pre-existing seed defect is supplemental; a newly violated
            // assertion or changed image is an authored committed observation.
            if old!=Some(row)||old.is_some_and(|r|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(r),r,prior)==Ok(true)){
                violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,"always");
            }
        }
    }
}
fn changed(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    assertions(bound,before,after,initial,viewer,steps,prior,current,out);
    if !super::super::census::body(&p.body){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: loop/opaque write boundary requires executed-effect tracing",p.name));return;}
    for inv in &bound.invariants{
        if matches!(inv.target.as_str(),"read"|"always")||inv.operation.as_ref().is_some_and(|op|op!=&p.name){continue;}
        let judged;
        let inv=if inv.target=="always"{inv}else{
            let Some(stable)=stability::necessary(bound,p,inv)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}: unknown/implicit policy changes need executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.target_label()));continue;};
            judged=stable;&judged
        };
        // A net image cannot identify an intermediate write or implicit effect.
        let writes=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if writes.len()!=1 {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}.{}: multiple or implicit writes require executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.table,inv.target));continue;}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1!="delete"&&before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|old|!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: commit-end/implicit retirement is not an executed delete",p.name));}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1=="delete"{
            for old in before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|old|!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(old,NOW)&&!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(t,_)|deps.contains(t)&&t!=&inv.table){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: delete policy dependency changes before disclosure",p.name));continue;}
                if prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(old),old,prior)==Ok(false){violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,old,Some(old),initial,"delete");}
            }
        }
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()&&!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(r,NOW));
            if old==Some(row){continue;}

//! Column worlds execute authored observers, including derived/count/refusal paths.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#121-column-read-intent — excluding a field from a projection must not produce a synthetic whole-row refutation.
use super::*;
fn reserve(out:&mut Campaign,cost:usize)->bool{
    if !out.reserve(cost){return false;}
    if out.column_observer_calls+cost>16384{out.unsupported.insert("column observers capped at 16384 call/world reservations; remaining value worlds not covered".into());return false;}
    out.column_observer_calls+=cost;true
}
fn discharged(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->bool{
    inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|kernel::implies(&super::super::columns::admission(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table],inv.column.as_deref()),p)==Ok(true))
        &&!bound.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|m.source==inv.table)
        &&!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).contains(&inv.table)))
        &&!bound.programs.iter().any(|p|ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(t,_)|t!=&inv.table)&&super::super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),&inv.table))
}
fn alternative(kind:&C,old:&Value)->Option<Value>{Some(match (kind.required(),old){
    (C::Text{max},Value::String(s)) if s.is_empty()=>if *max>0{Value::String("x".into())}else{return None},
    (C::Text{..},Value::String(s))=>Value::String(s.chars().map(|c|if c=='y'{'x'}else{'y'}).collect()),
    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|s|Value::String((*s).clone())!=*old)?.clone()),
    (C::Principal,Value::Id(s))=>Value::Id(if s==VIEWER{OTHER}else{VIEWER}.into()),
    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(n.checked_add(1)?),
    (C::IntRange{min,max},Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(if n<max{n+1}else if n>min{n-1}else{return None}),
    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
    _=>return None,
})}
fn sample_rows(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,inv:&Invariant,row:&Value,presence:bool)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>{
    let column=inv.column.as_ref()?;let kind=bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(column)?;
    let old=match row.field(column){
        Ok(value)=>value.clone(),
        Err(_) if kind.is_optional()&&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].defaults.get(column).is_none_or(|d|matches!(d,snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Null)))=>Value::Null,
        Err(_)=>return None,
    };
    let alt=if presence&&kind.is_optional(){if old==Value::Null{sample(kind.required(),0)?}else{Value::Null}}else{alternative(kind,&old)?};
    let mut out=rows.clone();
    let selected=out.get_mut(&inv.table)?.iter_mut().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())?;
    if let Value::Row(fields)=selected{fields.insert(column.clone(),alt);}else{return None;}
    Some(out)
}
fn same_base_world(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,primary:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,alternate:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,key:&Value)->bool{
    bound.schema.tables.iter().filter(|(table,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()).all(|(table,definition)|{
        let empty=Vec::new();let rows=primary.get(table).unwrap_or(&empty);let other=alternate.get(table).unwrap_or(&empty);
        rows.len()==other.len()&&rows.iter().all(|old|other.iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok()).is_some_and(|new|{
            let (Value::Row(a),Value::Row(b))=(old,new)else{return false;};
            a.keys().chain(b.keys()).filter(|c|!definition.maintained.contains_key(*c)&&!(table==&inv.table&&inv.column.as_ref()==Some(*c)&&old.field("id").ok()==Some(key))).all(|c|old.field(c).ok()==new.field(c).ok())
        }))
    })
}
// Counterfactual field mutation must not break an authored creator's copies
// or recipient guards. Direct typed payload observations run independently.
fn coupled(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->bool{
    let column=inv.column.as_deref().unwrap();
    let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
    if table.rules.insert.needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column])||table.update_rule_judged().needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column]){return true;}
    fn definitions(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>){
        if let Some(object)=value.as_object(){
            for tag in ["Const","Let","Assign"]{if let Some(binding)=object.get(tag)&&let (Some(name),Some(value))=(binding.get("name").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str),binding.get("value")){
                if tag=="Assign"||out.contains_key(name){out.insert(name.into(),serde_json::Value::Null);}else{out.insert(name.into(),value.clone());}
            }}
            for child in object.values(){definitions(child,out);}
        }else if let Some(xs)=value.as_array(){for child in xs{definitions(child,out);}}
    }
    fn fields<'a>(expression:&'a serde_json::Value,bindings:&'a BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,depth:usize)->Option<&'a serde_json::Map<String,serde_json::Value>>{
        if depth>32{return None;}
        if let Some(map)=expression.get("Object").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object){return Some(map);}
        if let Some(projection)=expression.get("Builtin")&&projection.get("builtin").is_some_and(|b|b.get("ProjectFields").is_some()){
            return fields(projection.get("args")?.as_array()?.first()?,bindings,depth+1);
        }
        if let Some(name)=expression.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str){return fields(bindings.get(name)?,bindings,depth+1);}
        None
    }
    fn dependency(value:&serde_json::Value,arg:&str,table:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,depth:usize)->bool{

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));
        // An independent denial must never consult a hidden read witness.
        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — an unreadable allocation cannot expose counter,
        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.
        if !self.sequence_insert_readable(table_name, &row)? {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "sequence read authority");
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "sequence read authority") });
        }
        if let Err(error) = supplied_sequence {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent upserts decide insert authority before field diagnostics.
            // A target key may supply an ordinal even when the patch does not.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
                return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
            }
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
        }

        } else { self.authorize_fields(table_name, &row, &supplied)?; }

            // only this first authority refusal is an undisclosed target miss.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? { return Err(self.no_write_target()); }
            if sequence_full { return Err(super::sequences::exhausted()); }
            // Engine event slots are stamped after predecessor/sequence checks,
            // just as on an ordinary insert; preflight validates authored fields.
            if let Err(error) = super::validate::validate_before_slots(self.schema, table_name, &row) {
                return Err(if matches!(error, RuntimeError::Input(_)) && !self.context.is_system() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
            }
        }
        if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule"));

  return messages where .conversationId = conversationId after c last 50 by .ordinal
```

Pass the returned `next` as `c` to page newest first by ordinal. Reusing the
mutation id on retry neither increments again nor inserts twice. This short
shared-history example is online-only and only members can advance its counter;
private conversations also need membership read rules. If you sync and predict it, a local ordinal is provisional until the
server commits and reconciles concurrent sends.

## Writing a member's read mark

Declare `readUpTo: int default 0 written by .principal` on the membership
row. `markRead` patches just that field; it needs no row update grant, but a
named `update (old, next) <- next.readUpTo >= old.readUpTo` still refuses a
backwards mark.
Other fields in the same patch require the row's update rule. Inserts by
another principal omit readUpTo, including when its intended value is 0.

The projection is private; append `readable by audience` for shared receipts.
Owners receive settled marks on every device, and held-row patches predict
offline. E_FIELD_AUTHORITY names the owner: omit the field or write as that
principal. See `guide rules` for the Clique membership recipe and private-field restrictions.

    pub(super) fn authorize_fields(&self, table: &str, row: &Value, supplied: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let fields = supplied.as_object()?;
        let mut owned = false;
        let mut ordinary = false;
        for field in fields.keys().filter(|field| field.as_str() != "id") {
            if let Some(authority) = definition.written_by.get(field) {
                owned = true;
                if !self.context.is_system() && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                    self.refusing_clause(table, &format!("field authority {field}"));
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", message: format!("{table}.{field} is written by .{}; omit {field} or write as the principal in .{} (guide rules)", authority.owner, authority.owner) });
                }
            } else { ordinary = true; }
        }
        Ok(owned && !ordinary)
    }

        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
        }}}
        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
        // Named primary keys used by role probes must select the challenged
        // viewer; changing the enum represents a current-role change while
        // derived assignment/membership rows remain present.
        if name!=inv.table {for (i,(n,k)) in t.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k,C::Principal)).enumerate(){fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Id(if variant & (1 << (i+3)) == 0 {VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into())); let _=k;}}
        out.insert(name,vec![Value::Row(fields.into())]);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
struct Snapshot<'a>{schema:&'a Schema,rows:&'a BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>}
impl Probe for Snapshot<'_>{
    fn exists(&mut self,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[Value],equalities:&[(String,Value)])->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
        let t=&self.schema.tables[table];let Some(idx)=t.index(index)else{return Ok(false)};
        if idx.components.len()!=key.len(){return Ok(false);}
        if idx.unique&&idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column().is_some_and(|n|t.columns.get(n).is_some_and(C::is_optional)))&&key.contains(&Value::Null){return Ok(false);}
        Ok(self.rows.get(table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|live(t,r)).any(|r|idx.components.iter().zip(key).all(|(c,v)|c.column().is_some_and(|n|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v)))&&equalities.iter().all(|(n,v)|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v))))

    /// Delete a row as the system principal: constraints, aggregates and
    /// partition events apply. Returns the number of rows removed (0 or 1).
    pub fn seed_delete(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {

    }
    /// Trusted deletion with the fixture/host clock, including publication and expiry.
    pub fn seed_delete_at(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str, now:i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_ephemeral_import(table)?;
        timeline::check_import(&self.schema, table)?;
        let now = self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {Ceilings::job()}else{Ceilings::unbounded()}, Cut::Abort), seq);
        if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {runner.reserve_bulk_source(table)?;}
        let removed = match runner.delete(table, id) {
            Ok(()) => 1,
            Err(RuntimeError::Require { .. }) => 0,
            Err(error) => return Err(error),
        };
        let events = runner.events.take();
        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
        drop(runner);
        if removed > 0 {
            tx.record_commit(seq, now, "seed", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "seed", &tables, removed)?;
            partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
        }
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(removed)
    }

    pub fn load_trusted(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {

    }

# Testing: fixtures and the packaged proof kit

Prove backend behavior with fixtures and the rendered UI with the packaged proof kit.
Starters generate frontend:proof and day2:proof, and the kit owns server and browser lifecycles.

```js
import { startDev, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
const dev = await startDev();
try { console.log(dev.url); } finally { await cleanup(() => dev.stop()); }
```

For shared test releases and probe:serve, use `guide staging`: real password users and operator grants on serve. Password-only dev fixture/clock tools require explicit --unsafe-dev-doors; startDev persists unless --memory is explicit.

Raw HTTP starts with JSON envelopes: POST /q/name `{args:{...}}`, POST /m/name
`{id:"stable-intent-id",args:{...}}`, POST /a/name `{id:"stable-action-id",args:{...}}`.
Keep the same id on an ambiguous retry. A mutation/action id identifies the attempt;
`result.id` identifies a returned row. Query data is under `data`, writes under `result`;
actions have their own lifecycle (`guide actions`), not a mutation receipt.

The generated frontend:proof builds its own dist with build (web) or build:web (Expo web).
After replacing the starter screen, replace its business assertions too.
`init --adapt` previews only and writes nothing; use the printed `--apply` command.
A custom Home has no write scenario: replace the Home assertions; a proof without a write fails.
`day2:proof` uses versions/v1 and versions/v2 (initial source snapshots and contemporary fixtures).

E_FIXTURE_ALIAS means a saved alias or path could not resolve. Use an earlier case's `save` name with `$name` or `$name.field`; escape a literal with `$$` or `$literal`. An unresolved assertion fails the case; it never compares the alias as literal data.

E_FIXTURE_TIME means a relative fixture time is malformed or out of range. Use `now` or `now±duration` with positive integral ms/s/m/h/d/w. Relative conversion applies only to typed time leaves; text/JSON strings stay literal.

## Refusal ladder

Supply every valid argument when testing authorization: decode (`E_INPUT`)
runs before the body. For a body ordered lookup → write → require, a hidden
asserted lookup refuses `NOT_FOUND`, a readable denied table write refuses
`E_RULE`, and only then can the later `require` emit its code. After decoding,
statements execute in authored order; put a require first to have it decide
first. Nullable hidden lookups return null; assertions use their authored
`else` code. Hidden denied update/delete targets retain NOT_FOUND. Earlier
writes roll back when a later require refuses.

A query over hidden rows is complete and empty when the pinned scope uniformly
refuses its viewer. A nonuniform scan can still be partial with no visible
rows. Test the complete flag as well as the count; do not expect E_RULE for a
hidden page. For a compiled role workflow see: `guide rule-recipes role-workflow`.
Use `guide client` for client recipes and the proof helper contracts below.

## Fixtures and cases

`test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. A fixture directory has
one `<table>.jsonl` per declared table, each line an object with `id` and fields:

```jsonl
{"id":"board-alice","owner":"dev:alice","title":"Groceries"}
```

Persona alice is `dev:alice`, never the literal `alice`. References use row ids;
time is Unix milliseconds or `"now"`, `"now-1h"`, `"now+2d"` in typed time fields
(including optional/list time leaves). Durations are positive whole ms/s/m/h/d/w.
Typed time arguments in cases accept the same forms.
One clock captured at test start anchors these values, seed defaults, cases and
the sweep, so timed fixtures do not rot; malformed/overflowing relative times
refuse E_FIXTURE_TIME. Text and JSON fields retain literal strings.
Money/decimal use integer minor units; bytes are hex; optional fields may be absent/null. Parents load before children.
Defaults, unique keys, caps, maintained values and sequences obey normal insert
semantics: omit sequence and engine-maintained columns. A missing parent is
NOT_FOUND; a bad row or unknown table fails the run before any case executes.

Add `test.cases.jsonl` to that directory. Cases execute in order; writes change
what later cases see. The complete vocabulary is `as`, `op`, `args`, `expect`,
`data`, `match`, `matchSet`, `count`, `save`, `label`; unknown keys fail before any case runs.

```jsonl
{"as":"alice","op":"addCard","args":{"board":"board-alice","text":"eggs"},"save":"eggs"}
{"as":"alice","op":"renameCard","args":{"card":"$eggs","text":"milk"}}
{"as":"bob","op":"cardsOn","args":{"board":"board-alice"},"count":0}
```

`save` names an answer: `$eggs` means its row id, `$eggs.text` its field; `$plans.0.id` selects the first row of a saved query list.
A bare id/reference/principal/cursor/asset argument equal to a saved alias refuses
with the `$name` rewrite (including optional/list leaves). Text, enum and JSON
arguments remain literals. A root `$alias` saved as null refuses E_FIXTURE_ALIAS
by name; `$row.optionalField` may be null.
Bare assertion strings (including enum literals) remain literals even if an alias has that name.
Aliases resolve recursively inside args, data and match. Unknown aliases and
missing fields refuse E_FIXTURE_ALIAS by name. `$$text` means literal
`$text`; `{"$literal":"eggs"}` means literal `eggs`, even if saved as an alias.
`data` asserts the exact result; `match` asserts a recursive object subset
(`"match":{"name":"Ash"}` permits an extra id). Arrays retain their exact
length/order, with each element matched recursively; scalars compare exactly.
`matchSet` compares a list without order: `"matchSet":[{"name":"Ash"},{"name":"Oak"}]`
requires exactly those two recursive subsets, each matched to a different row.
Duplicate counts matter. Nested arrays retain their order. All assertion forms may appear together. `count` asserts length. A mutation defaults to
`sent`; `ok` also accepts a sent mutation and never a refusal; a query defaults to its check verdict (`complete`, or complete/capped when
N_NEVER_COMPLETE). Set `expect` to `complete`, `capped`, `ok`, `denied`, or a
refusal code. `as` must be a declared persona; outsiders need their own persona.

After the cases, the sweep runs every query with that persona's case arguments when available,
otherwise derives arguments from the store after the cases' writes (text uses
the shortest valid value). Saved-alias arguments belong to the case run; the
sweep uses another literal-argument case or derives arguments for that query.

pub fn run(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    if options.flags.contains_key("release") {return run_forward::release(options);}

        return 2;
    };

//! Closed enum census: a new IR statement/expression/builtin forces review.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#3-sound-proof-boundary — opaque context changes are blockers.
use snapback4_core::ir::{Expr,Stmt,Builtin};
pub(super) fn body(xs:&[Stmt])->bool{xs.iter().all(|s|match s{
    Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Push{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value),
    Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>expr(condition)&&body(then_body)&&body(else_body),
    Stmt::For{..}=>false,
    Stmt::Require{condition,..}|Stmt::Check{condition,..}=>expr(condition),
    Stmt::Insert{row,..}|Stmt::InsertIfAbsent{row,..}|Stmt::Upsert{row,..}=>expr(row),
    Stmt::Update{key,patch,..}=>expr(key)&&expr(patch),
    Stmt::WriteTarget{patch,condition,..}=>expr(patch)&&condition.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|body(&c.setup)&&expr(&c.value)),
    Stmt::Delete{key,..}=>expr(key),Stmt::DeleteTarget{..}|Stmt::Break|Stmt::Continue=>true,
    Stmt::Emit{..}=>false,
})}
fn expr(e:&Expr)->bool{match e{
    Expr::Literal(_)|Expr::Variable(_)|Expr::Input(_)=>true,
    Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base),
    Expr::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().all(expr),Expr::Object(xs)=>xs.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>expr(base)&&fields.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>expr(left)&&expr(right),
    Expr::Block{body:xs,value}=>body(xs)&&expr(value),
    Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>r.key.iter().all(expr),
    Expr::Scan(_)|Expr::Merge(_)=>false,
    Expr::Builtin{builtin,args}=>pure(builtin)&&args.iter().all(expr),
}}
fn pure(b:&Builtin)->bool{match b{
    Builtin::JobAs|Builtin::JobQuery{..}=>false,
    Builtin::ProjectFields{..}|Builtin::ReceiptSettled{..}|Builtin::OptionalField{..}|Builtin::Calendar{..}|Builtin::Civil(_)|Builtin::Len|Builtin::Slice|Builtin::Concat|Builtin::Lower|Builtin::Trim | Builtin::Text(_)|Builtin::Sha256|Builtin::Join|Builtin::Group{..}|Builtin::First|Builtin::Last|Builtin::At|Builtin::Min|Builtin::Max|Builtin::Extremum{..}|Builtin::ExtremumOf{..}|Builtin::Divmod{..}|Builtin::Divide{..}|Builtin::Sort{..}|Builtin::MentionTokens|Builtin::SearchPresent|Builtin::RequireUncapped|Builtin::DeterministicShare|Builtin::Count|Builtin::Sum|Builtin::CountCapped|Builtin::Connected=>true,
}}

Two material false-positive blockers remain:

1. **Principal-keyed payloads still bypass authored creators.** [Roster classification](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/privilege.rs:117) treats an unrelated `role` column as authority when the table has `unique principal` or `unique principal,role`. [Generation](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/generated.rs:87) consequently invents payloads instead of executing the creator.

   Repro: a `reports` table with `principal`, `role: enum('draft','final')`, `approved: bool`, public read/insert, and this creator:

   ```quarry
   mutation file():
     return insert reports {principal: viewer, role: 'draft', approved: true}
   invariant reports always .approved
   ```

   Without a unique principal key, `test --fixture-only` passes. Adding either key produces four failing generated fixtures with `approved: false` and empty histories. The false refutation persists, and subsequent `check` exits 1. The committed payload-role control omits these keys.

2. **Generated bootstrap admission bypasses field authority.** [The admission loop](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/generated.rs:98) checks only the table insert gate, then uses trusted `seed_at`.

   Repro:

   ```quarry
   table docs:
     owner: principal
     mark: int default 0 written by .owner readable by audience
     read <- allow
     insert <- viewer = 'service:operator' and .owner != viewer
     online only
   invariant docs always .mark = 0
   ```

   `test --fixture-only` exits 1 with two generated fixtures containing `mark: 1`. No ordinary actor can insert those images: the table gate requires the operator, while supplying `mark` requires the different owner. An ordinary mutation supplying `mark: 1` refuses `E_FIELD_AUTHORITY`; omitting it succeeds with `mark: 0`. Generated initialization must enforce complete ordinary insert authority.

Two material false-positive blockers remain:

1. **Principal-keyed payloads still bypass authored creators.** [Roster classification](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/privilege.rs:117) treats an unrelated `role` column as authority when the table has `unique principal` or `unique principal,role`. [Generation](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/generated.rs:87) consequently invents payloads instead of executing the creator.

   Repro: a `reports` table with `principal`, `role: enum('draft','final')`, `approved: bool`, public read/insert, and this creator:

   ```quarry
   mutation file():
     return insert reports {principal: viewer, role: 'draft', approved: true}
   invariant reports always .approved
   ```

   Without a unique principal key, `test --fixture-only` passes. Adding either key produces four failing generated fixtures with `approved: false` and empty histories. The false refutation persists, and subsequent `check` exits 1. The committed payload-role control omits these keys.

2. **Generated bootstrap admission bypasses field authority.** [The admission loop](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/generated.rs:98) checks only the table insert gate, then uses trusted `seed_at`.

   Repro:

   ```quarry
   table docs:
     owner: principal
     mark: int default 0 written by .owner readable by audience
     read <- allow
     insert <- viewer = 'service:operator' and .owner != viewer
     online only
   invariant docs always .mark = 0
   ```

   `test --fixture-only` exits 1 with two generated fixtures containing `mark: 1`. No ordinary actor can insert those images: the table gate requires the operator, while supplying `mark` requires the different owner. An ordinary mutation supplying `mark: 1` refuses `E_FIELD_AUTHORITY`; omitting it succeeds with `mark: 0`. Generated initialization must enforce complete ordinary insert authority.

Validation passed: **21 language controls, 18 core ephemeral controls, eight CLI controls**, and the focused relationship-transfer control. This includes the real password-only mixed-ephemeral HTTP regression. I did not repeat the full histories target.

Later workspace edits appeared during review; neither findings nor test results qualify those edits. No repository files were modified by me. This verdict concerns the pinned source only; full cross-platform qualification remains separate, and no publication readiness is claimed.
